#5008. BNB Chain Launches 'Red Alarm' to Combat Rising Security Risks

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

BNB Chain has launched a new security feature, "Red Alarm," to help users identify potentially risky decentralized applications (dApps) and smart contracts in its ecosystem.

"Red Alarm" aims to improve user protection by highlighting potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, providing users with more information to consider before interacting with them.

BNB Chain said user safety in the crypto industry is a work in progress, especially as decentralized finance and blockchain applications are on the rise. As more users engage with smart contract systems and decentralized platforms, it has become increasingly important to identify potential security threats.

Keeping users safe takes ongoing work across crypto. BNB Chain’s Red Alarm contributes by flagging potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, giving users more information to work with. Explore the list 👇 https://t.co/FtWI1mWFVk https://t.co/hiTEVKyTyn

Through Red Alarm, users can access security-related warnings that may help them recognize suspicious applications and contracts before committing funds or approving transactions.

This measure follows after the Binance crypto exchange hack in late October put the entire crypto market on its toes, prompting crypto platforms to tighten their security.

On November 6, 2026, BNB Chain issued a notice, stating it was aware of the security risk affecting the Reth client. However, its opBNB does not use op-reth, with the impact on BSC-Reth being limited.

On the same day, BNB Chain announced a BSC-Reth hotfix, urging all BSC-Reth node operators on mainnet and testnet to upgrade to v0.1.4-hotfix as soon as possible and restart their nodes.

BNB Chain teases major developments, including its new L1 in development and continued upgrades on BNB Smart Chain (BSC).

Jenner is now on BSC testnet, bringing changes to payments, app timing, and token tools. BEP-703 introduces Payment Lane as part of the Jenner Hardfork on BNB Smart Chain (BSC). It reserves a share of each block's gas capacity for eligible payments, protecting that capacity from general traffic. The default reservation is 5% of each block's gas limit.

Payment Lane sets aside capacity for qualifying BNB transfers between wallets, so unrelated activity cannot use it all. Token payments can also qualify once approved for the lane.

#5007. HYPE loses $80 support as team distributions, institutional selling weigh on price — Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid [HYPE] fell below the $80-mark, a week after reclaiming the level as support. In fact, institutions are continuing to sell the altcoin too. Even though the team has been countering the selling pressure through token burns.

In the last 24 hours alone, HyperLabs distributed over 179K HYPE worth about $14.50 million to six team members’ wallets. The distribution was uneven, with these tokens unstaked about two days ago. These wallets are the ones to watch for potential selling pressure.

However, the protocol’s burn mechanism provided a counterweight to the new supply hitting the markets. Over 21K HYPE, worth about $1.64 million, was bought and burned at an average price of $77.93.

Meanwhile, a subsidiary of Hyperliquid Strategies Inc. (HSI) filed an application with the U.S Commodities Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) to become a Futures commission merchant. At the time of writing, the filing was still awaiting approval.

If approved, U.S clients could trade Perpetual Futures on a regulated platform, though there will be no definitive timing just yet. The HSI, meanwhile, reported $620.6 million in profits for the third quarter of this year. An approval would increase these profits in the next few sessions.

On the price charts, HYPE entered a downtrend, even though it was still confined in a sideways range. After Paradigm’s unstaking, the altcoin fell to around $80 and went on to break down the support. It established a low of $76 before entering a short pullback to the upside.

At press time, HYPE was trading towards the broken support at $80, potentially for a retest. Technically, it suggested that bears were in control, with the CMF showing capital flight as its reading stood at -0.19.

This reading was reinforced by the Directional Movement Index (DMI), with its three main lines all pointing south.

In short, HYPE has been facing conflicting signals lately.

Institutions have been unstaking despite looming regulatory clarity. Therefore, HYPE could hold above $75, a support level that has been in place for the last two months if bullish signals prevail.

Otherwise, if the level breaks down, it would set a precedent for demand zones at $70, $60, or lower.

#5006. Riot Sells More Than 20,000 Bitcoin as AI Data-Centre Contracts Reach $38B

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Riot Platforms says it sold 20,264 BTC in the first nine months of 2026, raising about $1.5 billion primarily for data-centre development. Its latest quarterly release puts long-term contracted data-centre revenue at around $38 billion, underlining how far the miner’s business model is shifting.

Riot Platforms has made a striking choice with its Bitcoin holdings: sell a substantial amount of the asset it mines to finance the infrastructure it hopes will deliver future revenue. That strategy is clearer than ever in its third-quarter results, released November 4.

In its earnings filing, Riot said it sold 20,264 Bitcoin year to date for approximately $1.5 billion, mainly to fund data-centre construction. The company also announced a 15-year powered-land lease for 910 critical IT megawatts at its Corsicana campus, representing about $28 billion of contractual value over the initial term.

Riot recorded $181.4 million in third-quarter revenue, barely above the comparable quarter a year earlier. Bitcoin mining contributed $116 million, down from $160.8 million, even though production increased to 1,637 BTC from 1,406 BTC. The company attributes much of the decline in mining revenue to a lower average realised Bitcoin price during the quarter.

At the same time, engineering revenue rose to $47 million and data-centre revenue was $18.4 million. Those lines make the transition visible in the income statement, although the extraordinary long-term lease figures are not the same as cash already earned. They depend on delivery milestones, counterparties, the terms of each agreement and years of operations.

Riot says it has now leased 1,151 MW of critical IT capacity, with roughly $38 billion of long-term contracted revenue. Investors should distinguish that total contract value from current profit, and consider what future capital expenditure may be required to bring the facilities online.

The Bitcoin sales create resources that can fund land, power connections, construction and specialised infrastructure. They also reduce direct exposure to future Bitcoin appreciation. It is a deliberate decision to exchange part of a liquid treasury asset for a business with potentially steadier contracted revenues, but one that carries construction and customer concentration risks.

Riot said it ended September with $950.2 million of cash and Bitcoin, including 2,418 BTC. It reported no equity raised during 2026, a detail relevant to shareholders concerned about dilution as build-outs expand.

Mining economics are still important. Riot reported an average cost to mine Bitcoin of $39,265 excluding depreciation, influenced by power-curtailment credits. Such credits are valuable, but energy arrangements and pricing can change. Our earlier look at Bitcoin miners’ growing AI commitments explored the same industry-wide tension between holding coins and building something new.

The milestones that matter most are construction progress, delivery of contracted megawatts, tenant payments and conversion of headline lease values into recurring revenue. A business can accumulate an impressive contract pipeline while facing sizeable financing, permitting or execution obstacles.

Riot’s disclosure shows that Bitcoin mining companies are not necessarily behaving like Bitcoin holding companies. One of the industry’s biggest operators is increasingly using the mined asset as a funding source for a different kind of data-centre business. That may eventually diversify earnings, but the timing and profitability have to be proven in cash flow rather than headlines.

This article was written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5005. Ripple Joins BlackRock, Standard Chartered in New Tokenization Report

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple contributed to a white paper commissioned by the Dubai Financial Services Authority (DFSA), which brings together perspectives and  insights from leading practitioners across the financial services ecosystem.

Ripple's Managing Director, Middle East & Africa, Reece Merrick, recently stated his contribution to  the DFSA's new publication: "Scaling Tokenization: The Road Ahead," alongside leaders from BlackRock, Standard Chartered and Robinhood.

The white paper considers the conditions that may support the responsible scaling of tokenised financial assets and related market infrastructure, including within the Dubai International Financial Centre (DIFC).

Aside from Merrick, other contributors include Tony Ashraf, Managing Director, Head of Technology & Operations and Head of Digital Assets, Middle East & Africa at BlackRock; Coy Garrison, Senior Director and Deputy General Counsel, Crypto at Robinhood; René Michau, Global Head, Digital Asset Advisory at Standard Chartered;  Henson Orser, Founder and Chief  Executive at Soter Insure; and Herman Schueller, Director, Innovation and Technology Risk Supervision at DFSA.

According to the DFSA report, tokenization of traditional financial assets has the potential to become a significant development in mainstream financial markets, offering the prospect of greater access for investors, increased efficiency for issuers and intermediaries, and reduced risk in certain areas, especially around settlement, for the wider financial system.

Merrick shared his perspective, saying that the real shift is not just tokenized treasuries and MMFs (Money Market Funds) gaining AUM but rather their on-chain utility as collateral in repo and margin without needing to redeem to cash first. This viewpoint follows as repo markets continue to move trillions daily, with Merrick highlighting this as potential for tokenization.

Merrick anticipates massive growth in this regard over the next 12 months, predicting that a significant proportion of that will be on the XRP Ledger.

"With clear frameworks, institutional custody, and trusted settlement assets like RLUSD, the UAE, DIFC and Ripple are positioned at the forefront of this next phase," Merrick stated.

Forecasts for the future size of tokenised assets vary considerably. Citi Institute's base case is for the global market in tokenised financial assets to reach $5.5 trillion by 2030.  A Standard Chartered and Synpulse paper estimated that demand for tokenised assets could reach $30.1 trillion by 2034.

#5004. Bitcoin Price Prediction: Can BTC Reclaim $82,000 as ETF Inflows Slip to $56 Billion?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Can Bitcoin climb back to $82,000? A recent forecast says BTC must reclaim that level before any real recovery, even as cumulative spot Bitcoin ETF inflows slip to about $56 billion.

That makes the next few weeks pivotal for any Bitcoin price prediction.

BTC trades in a narrow range well below its record high, and a possible Fed rate cut is the catalyst traders now watch.

Traders who position for that move also keep an eye on early-stage projects.

One of them is MEMASA ($MEMASA), an upcoming AI trading app that lets users research and trade crypto through a chat.

Bitcoin has drifted lower for a month, and a recent 24/7 Wall St analysis says the key test now sits near $82,000.BTC needs a sustained close above that level, the analysis argues, before any real recovery can take hold.

The main reason is weak fund flows, as cumulative spot Bitcoin ETF inflows have slipped to about $56 billion. Only a couple of weeks earlier, that total was higher, which means large investors have been taking money out rather than adding.

Monetary policy offers little help either, since the Fed’s benchmark rate recently moved up instead of down. Still, the analysis sees a return above $100,000 as plausible within the next couple of years, if ETF demand and Fed cuts line up.

Bitcoin now trades near $76,000, down almost 9% over the past month after a steady slide.

Nearest strong support sits near $73,500, and technical indicators lean bearish, so a retest of that floor looks likely.

For any near-term Bitcoin price prediction, losing that floor could open a path toward $70,000.

A move above resistance near $84,400, backed by returning ETF inflows, would change the picture for bulls.

MEMASA is an upcoming AI crypto trading app that lets users analyze markets, check token risks, and trade on-chain by chatting with an AI assistant.

The app tackles problems familiar to crypto traders: too many charts, scattered market data, scam tokens, and complicated interfaces. MEMASA plans to combine market intelligence, risk checks, automated strategies, and trading in one self-custodial app.

If the crypto market enters another bullish phase, growing interest in altcoins could bring more people into trading. MEMASA aims to serve that audience by making market research and trading as simple as a conversation.

Its potential appeal extends from beginners struggling with trading interfaces to experienced traders who want faster access to market insights.

$MEMASA is the utility token powering the MEMASA ecosystem. Its planned uses include premium AI intelligence, trading fee discounts, marketplace payments, and staking for analysts and AI agents.

The token’s potential derives from stronger sentiment across the crypto market and growing demand within the MEMASA ecosystem. If the app attracts a wide audience, it might drive the demand for $MEMASA.

MEMASA is currently offering early access to $MEMASA through an on-chain early sale, with the present stage set at $0.0155, below the project’s anticipated public listing price. Earlier stages offer lower prices, while later stages provide faster token unlocks.

A Bitcoin rally could create favorable conditions for MEMASA’s debut. The bigger test will be whether the app delivers on its promise to simplify crypto trading and attracts enough users to build lasting demand for its token.

Bitcoin’s next move hinges on whether buyers defend support near $73,500. A push back above $82,000, with ETF inflows returning, would give bulls their first real win.

BTC has still more than doubled over three years, so this slump follows a long climb. A Fed rate cut remains the catalyst to watch.

MEMASA faces a different test, as it needs to turn an AI trading concept into an app people use regularly. If it succeeds, demand for its utility token could grow with the ecosystem. Both Bitcoin’s path and MEMASA’s outcome remain uncertain, and higher potential comes with higher risk.

#5003. Dogecoin Contributor Flags Social Signing as Potential Wallet Security Risk

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Dogecoin community member Mishaboar has raised concerns about the security risks associated with social signing, a feature that allows users to create and access a blockchain wallet using everyday accounts like Google, Apple ID, Telegram, or email instead of managing a complex seed phrase.

This comes after Keplr, a multichain wallet supporting Cosmos, Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Starknet, stated it had received reports of unauthorized transactions on some Keplr accounts created with Google or Apple login.

The wallet provider stated that the incident has been escalated to the Web3Auth team, with both working together to investigate the matter.

Before Keplr's disclosure, Web3 wallet infrastructure provider Web3Auth said it was aware of a security issue affecting Web3Auth's services used by some third parties. It stated that it had temporarily paused the embedded wallet service in response to the issue; however, after an initial investigation, no evidence was found that MetaMask users were impacted.

In his X post, Mishaboar criticized social signing. "Social signing, the feature you may want to add to your wallet if you hate your users," Mishaboar wrote.

Social signing, the feature you may want to add to your wallet if you hate your users. The worst part is that the users that use the social signing option -and which will end up being hacked through it - are also often newbies, entering crypto for the first time. And the risk goes beyond the funds in your wallet - data leaks could link your real life identity to your holdings, which can have terrible consequences.

The concern is particularly significant for inexperienced crypto users who may not recognize the risks associated with connecting their personal accounts to wallet services. While the feature makes wallet creation and access easier, it could introduce security vulnerabilities that users may not fully understand, Mishaboar argued.

"The worst part is that the users that use the social signing option—and who will end up being hacked through it—are also often newbies, entering crypto for the first time. And the risk goes beyond the funds in your wallet—data leaks could link your real-life identity to your holdings, which can have terrible consequences," Mishaboar stated.

Mishaboar noted that this warning also extends to wallets using social sign-in, which allows users to back up their keys in the "Cloud," which just means somebody else's server, he explained.

This week, a security advisory was issued for iOS users, who are urged to update their devices to enhance their wallet security. This follows security researchers identifying two exploit kits, Coruna and DarkSword, which target devices running older iOS versions. These do not rely on a flaw in any wallet app, but once a device is compromised, they can access wallet data stored on it.

#5002. Hut 8 Expands AI Campus Commitments While Funding Buybacks With Bitcoin Sales

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Hut 8 says it fully commercialised its Beacon Point AI campus with a second 352 MW lease, taking expected aggregate base-term contract value across its AI data-centre portfolio to about $26.6 billion. The company also sold Bitcoin to fund share repurchases, showing how mining-era assets are being redeployed.

Hut 8’s third-quarter update is less about how many coins it mined and more about how much power, construction capacity and long-term tenant demand it can assemble. That represents a significant shift for a company long associated with Bitcoin infrastructure.

In its November 4 results, Hut 8 said a second 352-megawatt IT lease at Beacon Point fully commercialised the campus. It placed expected aggregate base-term contract value across its AI data-centre portfolio at about $26.6 billion and reported 1,330 MW of utility capacity under construction across its major AI projects.

The numbers describe commitments over years, not cash that has already appeared in the bank. Hut 8’s timetable includes an initial River Bend data-hall delivery target in the second quarter of 2027, followed by Beacon Point Phase 1 in the third quarter of 2027 and Phase 2 in the second quarter of 2028.

Those future dates matter. A contracted tenant can improve the business case for a development, but execution still depends on power availability, construction, testing, financing and customer readiness. The risk profile is not identical to Bitcoin mining, where machines may be deployed in stages and production responds directly to network conditions.

Hut 8 also reported 1,300 MW of additional energy capacity entering exclusivity, bringing that category to 3,180 MW. Exclusivity is a development milestone; it should not be interpreted as an operating facility or a guaranteed utility connection. Investors need to track the movement from a pipeline into actual delivered infrastructure.

The company said it bought back $74.7 million of shares during the quarter, funded with proceeds from selling approximately 954 Bitcoin. It later repurchased another $175.3 million of shares, funded through the sale of roughly 2,148 additional Bitcoin. Subsequent to the quarter, the repurchase authorisation increased to $1 billion.

That is a revealing capital-allocation decision. Selling Bitcoin to repurchase shares reduces treasury exposure to the coin while potentially increasing each remaining shareholder’s proportional interest in the company. Whether that creates value depends on the repurchase price and long-term performance of the business.

Hut 8 also closed a $1.07 billion senior secured revolving credit facility. The existence of a facility adds flexibility, but readers should distinguish committed borrowing capacity from cash revenue or money actually drawn. NewsBTC has previously examined the financing pressure created by miners’ AI expansion, and Hut 8 offers a clear example of how those choices reach the balance sheet.

Hut 8 has a substantial development pipeline and named delivery targets. The crucial tests will be whether those targets are met, whether project-level financing stays on acceptable terms and how much profit flows through when sites become operational.

The update captures a broader change in listed Bitcoin infrastructure businesses. Their market stories increasingly revolve around electricity access and long-term AI customers rather than the number of Bitcoin retained. That can produce a different revenue profile, but it does not remove the need for disciplined spending or independently verifiable execution.

#5001. 4-month low for XRP’s sentiment, but will Garlinghouse’s ‘North Star’ stay above $1?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

XRP’s price has been trading sharply towards $1 lately, with its price only a few cents away from hitting this psychological level at press time. Its weekly returns have been on the negative side too, explaining why the altcoin’s social sentiment fell on the charts.

XRP’s social sentiment hit its lowest level since July, with the total negative sentiment having a reading of 5.8435. The ratio of positive to negative sentiment was 0.65426, reflecting a growing bearish dominance.

The broader crypto market sentiment has also traded back to fear levels with the Fear and Greed Index at 32. This suggested that the shift in social sentiment might be extending beyond XRP.

The 4-hour chart seemed to be a true testament to the sharp decline in trader sentiment. The altcoin’s price fell from $1.32 to $1.15, breaking below a key support level at $1.24 amid a broader market sell-off.

The breakdown of $1.24 triggered massive long liquidations and stop losses totaling more than $10.23 million, according to CoinGlass. Its Open Interest (OI) also fell from $3.78 billion to $2.77 billion.

Stalling XRP ETF net inflows have worsened the situation too. This month, for example, the ETFs had only one day of outflows, with the rest of the days being inactive. All these factors precipitated XRP’s sharp descent to $1.15.

Still, all may not be lost, with XRP’s price still holding above $1.15. The buying volume exceeded that of sellers too, hinting at a breakdown in selling pressure.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse’s ‘North Star’ comments during the Ripple Swell event about XRP and the XRP Ledger have kept some optimism going around too.

CEO Brad Garlinghouse has repeatedly described XRP and the XRP Ledger as Ripple’s ‘North Star,’ which serves as the core purpose and guiding principle of the company.

This was his response in the context of whether the Ripple USD [RLUSD] stablecoin is shifting focus away from the chain’s native token, XRP. According to the exec, RLUSD is merely competing with other stablecoins.

Additionally, the $1.15-level was the last lower high before a market structure shift turned XRP bullish. Hence, the ongoing decline could just be a retest of the structure shift. Only time will tell if that’s true though.

In case XRP falls below the level, the next demand zone would be $0.98.

#5000. XRP Bulls Stunned by 3,090% Liquidation Imbalance as Crypto Market Stalls

According to data from the analytical platform Coinglass, $9.57 million in XRP positions were liquidated across exchanges over the past 24 hours.

Long positions accounted for $9.27 million compared with just $299,997 in short liquidations.

On the shorter scale, XRP liquidations amounted to $853,930, including $681,200 in long positions and $172,780 in shorts.

On Friday, XRP saw even bigger liquidations – $16.7 million in total, with $16 million in long liquidations. This event followed a massive market collapse on November 5, after the BTC price suddenly reversed its trend.

XRP is currently trading at $1.16, down 1.81% over the past 24 hours, while its market cap has declined 1.75% to $72.7 billion.

XRP's open interest is up only 1.4% over 24 hours, sitting at $1.37 billion. However, rising open interest does not automatically indicate that new bullish positions are being opened.

The trading volume has fallen 44.7%, which suggests limited recovery potential for today. In addition, XRP ETFs recorded zero net flows on Friday.

The current price level is close to the local bottom. The weekly chart hints at a further downtrend with $1.15 serving as the last support before the path to $1.00 is cleared.

Breaking this last support could trigger a liquidation cascade, resulting in almost a million dollars of positions closed.

The immediate resistance level is at $1.20. Breaking above it will be a clear indicator of an uptrend.

#4999. Core Scientific’s AI Colocation Revenue Jumps to $215M as Mining Role Shrinks

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Core Scientific reported $215.4 million in third-quarter colocation revenue, nearly 60% above the prior quarter, while billing 485 MW of capacity. Its results show how an operator once known for Bitcoin mining is building a much larger business around contracted AI infrastructure.

Some Bitcoin mining companies have talked about their artificial-intelligence ambitions for years. Core Scientific’s latest financials show what that transition looks like once facilities begin producing substantial revenue from customers.

According to its November 5 earnings release, the company generated $215.4 million of colocation revenue in the third quarter of 2026, up from $136.7 million in the second quarter and $15 million a year earlier. Total quarterly revenue reached $232.9 million.

Core Scientific said it was billing for 485 megawatts as of September 30, representing more than $700 million in average annualised colocation GAAP revenue. That is a different unit of operating performance from hashrate or Bitcoin produced. It describes contracted power and computing infrastructure delivered to tenants.

The move is important because mining revenue depends on several volatile factors at once: Bitcoin price, network difficulty, block rewards and electricity costs. Colocation can offer a different mix of long-term contracts, infrastructure obligations and construction spending. The income stream may be easier to forecast after a facility is delivered, but the capital commitment can be enormous.

Core Scientific says it is approaching full delivery under its first colocation agreement and is constructing capacity under a second agreement across five campuses. Those claims are meaningful milestones, yet remaining construction work and billing ramps still matter to the final financial outcome.

The company reported approximately $1.18 billion in quarterly net income, but said the result benefited from a non-cash change in the fair value of warrants. That makes the headline profit figure a poor stand-in for recurring cash generated by running data centres.

Adjusted EBITDA was $133.6 million, more than three times the previous quarter’s $41.1 million, while capital expenditure reached $985.1 million. Both figures deserve attention. A rapidly growing operating business can still consume substantial cash as it adds the facilities needed to earn future contracted revenue.

This is a useful counterpoint to the more familiar NewsBTC discussion of Bitcoin miners’ AI pivot. The opportunity is becoming measurable, but comparing revenue without spending, financing and delivery schedules would make the transformation look easier than it is.

Investors should follow the number of megawatts actually billing, customer diversification, operating margins, remaining capital needs and any modifications to lease terms. A nominal pipeline is less revealing than a site whose systems have been accepted by a paying tenant.

The results also offer a warning against treating every company in this sector as an identical bet on Bitcoin’s price. Core Scientific now has a material infrastructure business whose fortunes may depend more on demand for AI compute and construction execution than on daily mining profitability. Its history remains part of the story, but the metrics are changing underneath it.

#4996. Cipher Digital Starts Booking HPC Lease Revenue as $9.1B Contract Takes Shape

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Cipher Digital says it recognised its first high-performance-computing lease revenue in the third quarter of 2026 and extended its Barber Lake lease to 20 years, increasing contracted revenue to $9.1 billion. The milestone is about converting data-centre development into an operating business, not an instant cash windfall.

A signed data-centre lease and a functioning, revenue-producing facility are not the same achievement. Cipher Digital’s third-quarter update, released November 2, places the company at an important point between those stages.

In its official business update, the company said it recorded its first high-performance-computing, or HPC, lease revenue following the start of rent at Black Pearl. It also said the lease at Barber Lake had been extended to 20 years, raising its contracted revenue value to $9.1 billion.

The recognition of lease revenue shows that at least part of a project has advanced past development and into a billing relationship. It does not mean every planned data hall is operational. Cipher said multiple halls at Black Pearl and Barber Lake are scheduled to come online through year-end, so subsequent delivery remains central to the investment case.

High-performance computing infrastructure has different commercial characteristics from Bitcoin mining. Instead of purchasing machines primarily to win block rewards, the company develops sites intended to support customers’ demanding workloads under longer-term agreements. That can reduce some direct cryptocurrency market exposure while increasing dependency on construction schedules and individual tenants.

The length and total value of Barber Lake’s contract can help financiers evaluate future cash receipts. Still, contract value is not the same as revenue recognised in a quarter, cash available to shareholders or guaranteed profit. Operating expense, financing structure, maintenance and tenant performance all sit between those figures.

Cipher also reported conditional base-load classification for its 1-gigawatt Colchis project and early development of lateral pipelines intended to facilitate up to 2.5 GW of on-site generation for future expansion. These are large infrastructure ambitions, not facilities that have already delivered all their proposed capacity.

The central issue is access to reliable, affordable energy. Data-centre demand may be high, but sites cannot fulfil contracts without the supporting power network, cooling, equipment and permits. Companies that secured sites when they were focused on Bitcoin mining are trying to turn those physical advantages into AI capacity.

That shift has been a recurring theme in NewsBTC’s reporting on miners moving toward AI. Cipher’s results add a specific piece of evidence: its transition is no longer only a plan on a presentation slide, because some lease revenue has begun.

The next useful disclosures will show how much capacity has been accepted by customers, the revenue generated by each campus and the cost of completing construction. Readers should also check whether major contracts are concentrated among a small set of customers.

Cipher’s update is another reminder that Bitcoin-era data centres can develop into a very different asset class. Investors still need to judge whether the new use of that infrastructure will generate attractive returns after the enormous cost of building it. First lease revenue is a step forward, not the end of that assessment.

#4993. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.

#4992. 4 Korean Deals Accelerate Ripple’s Institutional Push

Home » Trendsetter News » 4 Korean Deals Accelerate Ripple’s Institutional Push

South Korean financial institutions have signed agreements with Ripple, testifying to a growing interest in digital-asset custody.

Four South Korean financial institutions have signed agreements with Ripple within six months, according to Kamilah Stevenson, a wealth-focused market commentator tracking the company’s expansion.

Her latest video points to an insurer, an internet bank, a regional lender and a securities firm as evidence of growing institutional interest, but their involvement does not necessarily translate into XRP demand.

The most consequential detail is the emphasis on custody. Three of the four arrangements begin with infrastructure for safeguarding digital assets, rather than cryptocurrency trading or payments.

Dr. Kamilah Stevenson says Kyobo Life agreed in April to test holding, transferring and settling tokenized Korean government bonds using Ripple Custody. The stated objective is to shorten settlement from roughly two days to something closer to immediate execution.

K Bank followed at the end of April, selecting Ripple Custody to develop digital-asset wallets for institutional clients, according to the video. A regional bank then adopted Ripple Payments in August for cross-border business transfers.

The fourth agreement involves Meritz Securities, which the commentator describes as Ripple’s first Korean securities-firm partner. Its work will focus on custody and tokenization, with potential interests including spot crypto ETFs, fractional investments, security tokens and won-backed stablecoins.

“Where does it sit and who’s responsible for keeping it safe?” the host asks, arguing that custody approvals lay the groundwork for subsequent products. That is the video’s central thesis: institutional adoption starts with operational controls, not necessarily a tradable token.

Dr. Kamilah Stevenson identifies February 4, 2027, as the effective date for new Korean legislation covering tokenized securities, and cites a November 11 deadline for public comments on proposed rules. Those dates are presented as a potential catalyst, although the transcript does not independently establish the regulatory timetable.

For XRP investors, the distinction between Ripple’s business and XRP usage crucially matters. Ripple Payments can use XRP for currency conversion, but the YouTube video does not establish that the Korean bank’s payment flows actually do.

Sharing a ledger, however, does not mean every exchange between stablecoins must route through XRP. The agreements suggest broader institutional interest in digital-asset infrastructure; their effect on XRP demand will depend on which services reach production and how transactions are settled.

#4991. Circle’s USDC Transaction Volume Rises 49% Even as Reserve Income Faces Pressure

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Circle reported $13.9 trillion of USDC onchain transaction volume in the third quarter of 2026, up 49% year over year, while average USDC circulation reached $73.3 billion. The issuer’s results show strong network activity, but that does not translate directly into equivalent revenue growth.

Stablecoin businesses are often judged by a single number: how many dollars’ worth of tokens are circulating. Circle’s latest earnings suggest that approach misses a substantial part of what is happening around USDC, and also oversimplifies how the company makes money.

In its November 4 SEC-filed results, Circle reported $13.9 trillion in USDC onchain transaction volume for the quarter, an increase of 49% from a year earlier. Average USDC in circulation rose 8% to $73.3 billion, while total revenue and reserve income increased 17% to $865 million.

A stablecoin can move between users many times without the amount outstanding increasing. That makes onchain transfer volume a useful measure of network activity but a poor substitute for payment revenue, spending by real-world customers or income retained by the issuer.

Some onchain activity also reflects exchange settlement, liquidity management and transfers between controlled addresses. It would be a mistake to present $13.9 trillion as a tally of retail purchases or to assume the growth rate proves stablecoins have replaced ordinary banking payments.

Circle’s net income from continuing operations reached $226 million, up 5% year on year. Adjusted EBITDA increased 85% to $317 million. Differences in the growth rates reflect a changing mix of operational, distribution and accounting factors; investors should assess the underlying disclosures rather than treating all three numbers as interchangeable signals.

Circle highlighted that its payments network reached $41.3 billion in annualised total payment volume during the quarter. It also announced plans to acquire Tazapay, a cross-border payments platform, with completion expected in 2027 subject to approvals. These initiatives aim to make it easier for businesses to move between traditional payment systems and digital-dollar settlement.

This strategy faces a practical challenge: issuing a token is relatively simple compared with connecting a broad set of banking, compliance and payout arrangements. Customers require dependable redemption, useful local currency access and clear rules. Onchain transaction volumes alone do not tell us whether those services are profitable.

The competitive environment is also changing. As NewsBTC noted when some stablecoins faced new European trading restrictions, distribution is shaped by regulation as well as technology. An issuer needs both.

Beyond the headline USDC supply, the key numbers are revenue after distribution costs, payment volumes generated by identifiable services, changes in the reserve-income environment and the cost of expanding infrastructure. Circle has shown activity and profit growth, but it must continue converting that network usage into durable economics.

The bigger story is a maturing business model. Stablecoins increasingly compete as financial infrastructure, not simply as exchange trading pairs. Circle’s quarter offers evidence that USDC is moving at scale, while also reminding readers that volume, adoption and shareholder returns are three different questions.

#4990. Bitcoin price prediction — With $80K lost, is more downside ahead for BTC?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Despite rebounding slightly above the $80,000-level, Bitcoin failed to hold above this level. In fact, it has since fallen to the $75K-level, where it has been consolidating for the last three days.

This relief rally was partly driven by liquidation of sell orders, which in turn produced a short squeeze. According to a CryptoQuant report, more than $290 million in shorts were wiped out, amplifying the uptick.

Furthermore, the stablecoin supply fell by about 5% over the past month, implying part of the capital was being swapped for BTC among other cryptos.

For instance, the Drift Protocol hacker went long after swapping $13 million USD Coin [USDC] on Hyperliquid for Bitcoin. Some large wallets were also accumulating the token even though the apparent demand remained negative, showing the market did not absorb available BTC.

However, Bitcoin did not hold above $80K despite this mild buying activity. This observation could be attributed to a couple of on-chain factors.

The report further observed that spot demand has remained weak, as the monthly average net spot volume delta was at -$28 million and -$9.40 million on Binance and Coinbase, respectively.

In addition, short-term holders have been moving their holdings to exchanges this month. While these transfers of about 32.70K BTC do not prove sales, they do imply looming selling pressure.

Moreover, institutional demand through BTC ETFs fell flat, with $412 million in net outflows recorded. The largest single outflow came from BlackRock’s IBIT, where traders sold $403 million in BTC.

Further analysis of the Hyperliquid exchange revealed that whales have been adding shorts. The net positioning turned bearish after seven months of being bullish.

The last time BTC had such a huge short exposure was in October 2025, when the price crashed to $60K thereafter. These bearish bets have simply added to the pressure.

Finally, the Coinbase Premium Index, which tracks both Coinbase and Binance for the U.S market, has stayed negative.

BTC’s price action at press time revealed a sideways consolidation since the crash to $75,000. The returning mild accumulation could offset bearish positioning and sustain a recovery.

However, there need to be improvements in the apparent demand, ETF inflows, and U.S buying pressure.

#4989. Ripple's Schwartz Speaks Out About His Influence Over XRP Price

David Schwartz, former chief technology officer of Ripple and one of the original XRP Ledger architects, has responded to claims that he is partially to blame for XRP's poor performance.

In a recent interaction on the X social media platform, David Schwartz was challenged about his possible impact on the price of the Ripple-linked cryptocurrency.

"Don't run from this. You can honestly say you don't have anything to do with it? It's not unfounded anger," the user wrote.

Although David Schwartz does not entirely deny that he can have an impact on the price of XRP, he is clear that it is minimal.

"Can I honestly say that I have nothing to do with the price of XRP? I guess probably not nothing. But very little," David Schwartz responded.

The response from Schwartz comes in the wake of mounting anger within the XRP community about the token's long-running price slump.

Indeed, XRP has seen roughly a 50% decline in value over the past year, per data from CoinMarketCap. During this period, the token has seen several temporary spikes, but the overall trend has been downward.

Of course, XRP is not the only token experiencing such a dramatic drop in value.

Stellar (XLM), another prominent cryptocurrency that seeks to enable payments, has seen a very similar price trend over the same time period.

The interaction came on the back of a long post in which Schwartz lamented the rise in hostile replies he receives on X.

Schwartz, a Ripple veteran, disclosed that some users are actually following him into other discussions and then proceeding to either label him a fraud or criticize him for his work on XRP.

The Ripple veteran noted that he has now made the decision to block such users, as irrelevant crypto-related posts are disrupting his discussions with other users.

Nonetheless, he stressed that the decision affects both critics and fans. Even if someone makes a positive comment about XRP but in an unsuitable place, they might also get blocked.

He also emphasized that he has no issue with honest criticism or accusations that make sense and come with good arguments.

He is mostly annoyed with users who keep hijacking unrelated discussions to vent their frustration.

In fact, Schwartz even offered to unblock users who commit to not doing so in the future.

Schwartz stepped away from his day-to-day duties at Ripple at the end of 2025. He had worked at the firm for over a decade. However, he has not completely left Ripple.

Earlier this month, Schwartz clarified that he's still on Ripple's board of directors and holds the honorary title of CTO Emeritus.

Furthermore, he disclosed that he still meets with his old team twice a month.

#4988. BVNK Opens OUSD Minting and Payments Across Four Blockchains

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: BVNK says eligible business customers can now mint, redeem, hold and transfer Open USD (OUSD) on Ethereum, Solana, Base and Tempo. The November 6 rollout is a practical payments integration, but the company has not provided transaction volumes proving how extensively the new service is being used.

A stablecoin’s biggest hurdle may not be issuing tokens but making them useful inside ordinary financial operations. Payments infrastructure company BVNK has taken a step in that direction by making Open USD accessible through its existing platform.

In a November 6 announcement, BVNK said eligible businesses can mint and redeem OUSD against dollar balances, hold it and transfer it on Ethereum, Solana, Base and Tempo. The product is aimed at payment providers, treasury teams and companies that need to move between bank money and blockchain settlement without assembling separate integrations for every network.

BVNK outlines three routes. A direct integration allows a company to use OUSD in its own treasury and operations. An embedded option lets developers offer wallets, pay-ins or payouts within customer-facing applications. Wholesale access is aimed at exchanges, trading desks and market makers that need larger-scale minting and redemption.

These categories are more important than a promotional claim of being multichain. A business needs clarity about the currency held, when redemption is available, which network is supported and whether the underlying legal and compliance arrangements suit the countries involved. A token arriving quickly on a blockchain is only one step in an end-to-end payment.

BVNK says the launch offers 1:1 conversion against dollar balances and positions OUSD as part of an open-infrastructure model. The firm identifies a consortium of financial and technology companies behind the wider initiative, but membership of a consortium should not be mistaken for a guarantee of liquidity, issuer credit quality or operational support from every named institution.

A company with customers on different chains can otherwise face bridge complexity, additional custody arrangements and fragmented liquidity. Supporting several networks in one interface potentially reduces that operational burden, especially for businesses already using BVNK’s services.

However, crosschain availability does not mean every route is interchangeable or that every address and wallet supports every version of the token. Businesses still have to prevent wrong-network transfers and reconcile balances across chains. The value of the integration will be visible in reliability and error handling, not just a list of supported networks.

For context, NewsBTC has covered how Solana’s settlement tools are being built for institutions. OUSD addresses a related but separate problem: supplying a payment asset and an operating layer through which companies can use it.

BVNK says OUSD is available to eligible business customers outside the European Economic Area. That geographic qualification matters because stablecoin access and licensing rules differ between jurisdictions. Users should check eligibility before assuming they can open a new account or send tokens through a particular network.

The company has not published figures establishing how much OUSD has been minted or transferred specifically through this new integration. For now, the news is a live product capability, not a demonstrated leap in adoption. The next useful evidence would be sustained payment volume, repeat business usage and disclosures about redemption performance during busy markets.

#4987. Solana’s Faster Finality Fails to Halt SOL’s Price Slide

Home » Trendsetter News » Solana’s Faster Finality Fails to Halt SOL’s Price Slide

Solana’s Alpenglow upgrade reportedly cut transaction finality to roughly 400 milliseconds, but still can’t pick SOL’s price up.

Solana transactions reportedly reached finality in roughly 400 milliseconds after its Alpenglow upgrade, yet SOL fell after the switch. In the supplied video, a crypto analyst examines that disconnect: a faster network may improve its appeal to payment providers without immediately giving investors a reason to buy.

Fire Hustle places the upgrade’s launch on October 21 and describes SOL trading near $100 afterward. The YouTube video’s central argument is that technical progress remains secondary to broader market conditions and investment flows when it comes to short-term prices.

According to her, Alpenglow replaced Solana’s TowerBFT voting system, moving validator votes off-chain and reducing finality from about 12.8 seconds to roughly 400 milliseconds in the first day’s data. That remains above the team’s stated 150-millisecond target.

The transition required validators representing at least 82% of staked SOL to approve the new system before user transactions resumed. The analyst describes a brief pause rather than a reported outage, following months of test-network rehearsals.

There is also a software-diversity concern. The YouTube video episode says Firedancer validators could not participate in the switch and temporarily had to return to the main client, leaving the network more dependent on one implementation.

Moving votes off-chain also changes the transaction count displayed by block explorers. “Check what’s actually being counted before you panic,” she says, warning that lower headline transactions-per-second figures need not mean user activity has collapsed.

Fire Hustle reports 12 consecutive trading days of outflows from Solana ETFs and describes October as their worst month for withdrawals since launch. It also cites a roughly 12% weekly decline in open leveraged SOL positions on OKX.

Corporate buyers offered a counterpoint. According to Fire Hustle, DeFi Development Corp. purchased another 22,070 SOL during the decline, while Forward Industries added approximately 949,000 SOL in the quarter ending September.

On the chart, she watches support in the mid-$90s and an initial recovery zone around $110–$115. Those are conditional trading levels, not price forecasts: a weekly break below support could expose the $80s.

In a nutshell: faster settlement could support payment adoption, but sustained usage and a reversal in fund outflows would offer firmer evidence that the upgrade is translating into market demand.

#4986. $200 billion lost, but here’s why Solana’s tokenized RWA market could be silver lining

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Altcoins have been bleeding more than Bitcoin [BTC], even though the latter is the main driver of the crypto market’s sentiment. In total, altcoins have lost more than $200 billion in capitalization, implying that the euphoria around them was short-lived.

Deducing Binance’s market, over 70% of the altcoins broke below the 200-day moving average (MA). Here, it’s worth noting that this share is quickly approaching the 80% mark. For instance, at press time, Solana [SOL] was trading at $89 with the MA positioned at $97.76.

Solana slid after the midterm elections’ outcome, exerting pressure on the altcoin. At press time, SOL was 9.75% away from its 200-day MA. This reading indicated that bears were in control of the market.

Even so, traders are betting on the growing RWA market to help mitigate the downside risk. As such, SOL could be an outlier when the general altcoin market is weak. Bitcoin would also need to be bullish for more confluence though.

#4985. Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Four flaws in Bitcoin’s consensus rules that have lingered for years.

Issues like quantum resistance may command attention, but a practical cleanup is what many experts think Bitcoin’s next soft fork is likely to address, spanning fixes for vulnerabilities in the network’s difficulty adjustment mechanism to denial-of-service vectors among miners.

Proposed in 2019 and known as the Great Consensus Cleanup (BIP-54), the upgrade seeks to address the “cruft” in Bitcoin’s consensus design through changes that have historically been sidelined, as described by long-time Bitcoin developer and BIP-54 co-author Matt Corallo.

“There’s a laundry list of things we know of that we really need to clean up,” he said during a recent appearance on MARA Foundation TV. “All of these issues are real concerns that could impact Bitcoin negatively, but they’re not like, ‘Oh, the sky is falling.’”

Although a formal timeline for the BIP’s undertaking hasn’t been established, Corallo thinks there’s an 80% chance that the Great Consensus Cleanup is up next for the network. During his talk with Head of MARA Foundation Isabel Foxen Duke, he also called it a practical exercise for Bitcoin developers who haven’t yet been through a soft fork.

At its core, Corallo said BIP-54 “fixes some stuff that everyone agrees are problems,” while targeting four specific vulnerabilities:

Still, not everyone views a maintenance-focused soft fork as a major win. On X, Paul Sztorc, co-founder and CEO of LayerTwo Labs, called BIP-54’s potential activation “kind of depressing, since it is a janitorial maintenance fork that doesn't do anything.”

Surveying developers at TABconf in Atlanta, Sztorc noted that Bitcoin’s “best and brightest” predominantly expect the Great Consensus Cleanup to be Bitcoin’s next soft fork anyway, with the earliest estimate in September 2027, and most pointing to 2028 or later.

Following the activation of Taproot in 2021, Bitcoin’s community has faced years of gridlock surrounding protocol changes. This friction is frequently framed around “ossification,” with broader protocol changes like OP_CAT (BIP-347) and OP_CHECKTEMPLATEVERIFY (BIP-119) struggling to reach ecosystem-wide consensus.

According to Corallo, soft forks have always involved overcoming technical hurdles. But more recently, he’s observed political and social friction that’s become just as relevant.

The Bitcoin community spent months debating how Taproot should be activated, with arguments pitting miner-signaling thresholds against mandatory user flag days. Corallo argues that BIP-54 can establish a deployment playbook for future soft forks.

“In every recent soft fork, there’s also been a bunch of drama about activation mechanisms,” he said. “So hopefully we could do a soft fork to go through that drama, and then copy and paste and reuse it and head off some of that drama [for future soft forks].”

In that sense, BIP-54 is just as much about proving Bitcoin can still agree on upgrades at the social layer as it is about actual code cleanup.

André Beganski is a Senior Content Manager at MARA Foundation, producing content on topics including the intersection of quantum computing and cryptography. He has previously worked for Decrypt.

#4984. Binance's CZ Calls for Crypto Industry Unity

Former Binance CEO Changpeng Zhao (CZ) is pleading for more unity in the cryptocurrency space, claiming that bickering between different crypto factions doesn't make much sense as digital assets are tightly connected.

"Most crypto assets go up and down together. % might vary a bit, but what's happening to one affects everyone. In-fighting in this space makes zero sense," CZ tweeted.

He capped the post with a simple message pleading for unity: "We need to be: decentralized but united."

CZ was reacting to a social media post that pointed out how crises involving exchanges in the past had affected their respective tokens' prices.

The post pointed to the FTX fiasco in 2022, the WazirX hack in 2024, and the recent security breach at Bitget as examples of how troubles at exchanges can result in significant market losses.

The discussion even pointed to the massive hack at Binance on Oct. 29, which makes CZ's plea for more unity across the crypto space all the more relevant.

The crypto space is currently grappling with one of the biggest security breaches in recent memory.

Binance suffered a staggering $6.34 billion hack on Oct. 29 after hackers broke into the exchange's internal wallet signing infrastructure.

Several digital assets, including Bitcoin, Ethereum, XRP, and Solana, were all impacted by the hack.

Binance has assured users that it will bear the losses from the hack, as opposed to passing them along to customers. According to the exchange's update shared on Nov. 3, around $2.85 billion in stolen funds have been frozen or intercepted, while 98% of affected token assets have been replenished.

In recent days, CZ has been quite vocal about cybersecurity threats.

On Nov. 5, he advised crypto traders to revoke API keys for the third-party trading platform SignalPlus after it was reported that there might have been a security breach.

Meanwhile, CZ also warned his followers against fake websites posing as Trust Wallet, stressing that people shouldn't mistake domains ending in ".cam" for legitimate ".com" ones.

Furthermore, he asked iPhone users to ensure that their OS is up to date following reports of vulnerabilities that could leave crypto holders susceptible to malicious attacks.

#4982. Roger Ver Buying Bitcoin Cash Again? Suspicious $163,000 Swap Raises Eyebrows

A mysterious $163,000 crypto transaction has fueled rumors that Roger Ver, the controversial Bitcoin evangelist also known as 'Bitcoin Jesus,' may be purchasing Bitcoin Cash (BCH) once more.

According to on-chain analytics platform Onchain Lens, 73.31 Ethereum (ETH) coins were recently swapped for around 655.74 BCH coins (around $163,000) with the help of the decentralized cross-chain protocol THORChain.

Ver was famously an avid proponent of BCH, hailing it as a better option than the original Bitcoin network.

However, Onchain Lens didn't offer definitive proof that the wallet in question belongs to Ver.

Ver, a crypto pioneer who first entered the crypto space in 2011, quickly rose to become one of the most recognizable early Bitcoin advocates.

He was so passionate about the digital asset that he even earned the moniker 'Bitcoin Jesus' for his aggressive promotion of the cryptocurrency.

But as Bitcoin's controversial scaling debate (which ultimately saw the birth of Bitcoin Cash in August 2017) kicked off, Ver's relations with Bitcoin's mainstream community took a hit.

Ver quickly emerged as one of Bitcoin Cash's most vocal champions.

He passionately argued that BCH's larger block sizes and lower fees help keep Bitcoin true to its roots as a peer-to-peer electronic cash system.

For the past year, Roger Ver has been in the news mostly due to his well-publicized legal dispute with the U.S. government.

Back in April 2024, the crypto entrepreneur was arrested in Spain after U.S. authorities accused him of failing to pay roughly $48M in taxes on his massive Bitcoin holdings and transactions.

Ver, who renounced his U.S. citizenship back in 2014, insisted the prosecution was unjust.

He persistently tried to poke holes in the government's argument.

In June 2025, Ver upped the ante even further.

He took Spain to the European Court of Human Rights to contest the country's decision to extradite him to the U.S.

But the feud took a bombshell twist in October 2025.

Ver and the U.S. Justice Department reached a settlement through a deferred prosecution agreement.

The cryptocurrency figure agreed to pay almost $50 million in unpaid taxes, penalties, and interest.

US spot Bitcoin and Ether exchange-traded funds (ETFs) posted about $530 million in net outflows Thursday as cryptocurrency markets fell sharply.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) recorded another $105 million in net outflows. The latest withdrawals extended IBIT’s outflow streak to five consecutive sessions, with more than $1 billion withdrawn since Oct. 30.

#4979. Web3Auth Exploit Doesn't Affect Trust Wallet: Products That Might be Affected

Crypto industry is dealing with another security turmoil, as a breach at Web3Auth, the wallet authentication infrastructure owned by Consensys, has exposed private keys of certain Keplr wallets, according to the official statement of TrustWallet representative on X.

The November 7 incident led to emergency service suspensions and recovery efforts, which could add pressure to an already fragile crypto market.

Web3Auth confirmed that a security issue in its infrastructure had compromised certain embedded wallets integrated with Keplr, especially accounts created through Google or Apple login.

Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery estimates that attackers drained at least $6 million from more than 44,000 wallets.

Those wallets sit across eight Cosmos ecosystem networks, including Cosmos Hub, Osmosis, Celestia and Injective.

Keplr warned users from depositing into compromised accounts and Web3Auth on their side suspended embedded wallet service, actively working on a recovery of vulnerable assets.

MetaMask stated that there is no evidence of affected users, though the situation is still under control. Trust Wallet has been unaffected, says its representative Felix. The impact on other third-party integrations is yet to be confirmed.

On October 29, Binance reportedly suffered a massive security breach involving around $6.3 billion, while Bitget was hit by an exploit estimated at $387.5 million in September.

Earlier in 2026, KelpDAO lost around $290 million after an exploit involving cross-chain infrastructure.

DeFi protocols, wallet infrastructures, smart contracts: those kinds of incidents are relevant for almost every project on the market. Exploits directly affect market sentiment, investors losses and safety in the industry in general.

Projects that rely on embedded wallet technology and Cosmos-related assets have unfortunately been affected directly and repeated security failures might damage their market picture state even further.

Luckily, there's no proven link between the Web3Auth expoit and declines on the market as a whole, including assets like Bitcoin and Ethereum. The investigations and rapid protocol patches are being released after more than 24 hours since the discovery.

#4978. Robinhood Chain was built for tokenized stocks – But memecoins took over

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Gen Zs are rewriting the rules of wealth creation, increasingly trading the traditional financial playbook for a high-stakes game of chance.

From cryptocurrencies and memecoins to sports betting and prediction markets, the generation is exploring unconventional ways in pursuit of financial gains that once seemed possible only through years of saving and investing.

In this complex financial landscape, the line separating investing from gambling is blurring by the day. In fact, Ashley Embers recently highlighted how 37% of Gen Z respondents engage in sports betting, while 20% place bets online.

More concerningly, over half of Gen Z adults aged 18–29 reportedly redirected money originally intended for investments into sports betting over the past year, with 14% doing so several times a month.

This shift is also being reflected in a few corners of the crypto market, which is even better than memecoins.

One interesting example of this shift is Robinhood Chain.

When Robinhood introduced its Ethereum [ETH] Layer-2 network, the company planned to build a three-in-one infrastructure layer, which includes tokenized stocks, real-world assets [RWAs], and decentralized finance [DeFi] applications.

Expanding on this, Johann Kerbrat, SVP and General Manager of Crypto and International at Robinhood, said,

We’re bringing the best of traditional finance and DeFi together, and in doing so, expanding financial ownership to every corner of the globe.

The main goal of the team was to bring traditional financial markets on-chain to enable users to access tokenized versions of familiar assets through blockchain infrastructure.

However, the network’s early adoption took a complete U-turn from the initial goal. Rather than being primarily driven by tokenized equities and traditional financial products, Robinhood Chain attracted considerable attention from memecoin traders.

Expressing his opinion on the matter, Robinhood CEO Vlad Tenev said,

If you hold a memecoin, you just get stock tokens airdropped to you.

This has raised an interesting question: Is Robinhood building the next generation of tokenized financial markets, or has it accidentally created another destination for speculative crypto trading?

Tokenized equities were expected to be one of the Robinhood’s defining use cases. However, the initial trading activity suggested that traders have found another reason to use the network.

Per Bitquery’s analysis of Robinhood Chain’s trading activity between the 16th and the 28th of July 2026, memecoins accounted for approximately 93.5% of trading across 63.5 million transactions. Tokenized equities, meanwhile, represented just 6.5%.

CoinGecko also highlighted this shift in trend. As per its July 2026 assessment, memecoins accounted for 79.2% of Robinhood Chain’s DEX volume, whereas RWAs made up just 9.69%.

These reveal a considerable gap between Robinhood’s intended use case and actual market demand.

Robinhood Chain has also enabled creators to pair their memecoins with tokenized versions of established companies.

This means that tokenized equities are not necessarily being used for traditional investment purposes. Instead, they have become the trading currency through which users speculate on unrelated digital assets.

Similarly, Bloomberg reported that daily trading volume in pairs combining Robinhood stock tokens with memecoins had reached approximately $443 million in early September.

Yet, despite the speculative nature of its activity, Robinhood Chain has recorded impressive growth since its launch. CoinGecko’s Q3 2026 Crypto Industry Report highlighted the network as the third-most-active blockchain within just three months of its public launch.

In Q3 2026, Robinhood Chain generated more than $88.9 billion in trading volume, averaging approximately 9.5 million transactions per day.

Daily trading volume on its native decentralized exchanges also increased from approximately $1.3 million on launch day to a peak of $3.5 billion on the 4th of September.

The company also added approximately $25 million worth of Bitcoin to its corporate balance sheet in October, describing the purchase as an asset-diversification strategy.

These developments indicate that Robinhood’s broader ambitions extend well beyond memecoins.

However, memecoin trading on the Robinhood Chain is not without its risks.

Bitquery’s investigation found that approximately 84% of the nearly 15,000 tokens launched daily never traded again after their initial transaction. The investigation also revealed that three out of every four open trading positions were underwater.

Meanwhile, Fortune subsequently reported that trading volume in these memecoin-stock pairs had fallen by approximately 96% from its early-September peak.

This highlights how quickly speculative enthusiasm disappears. Yet, despite these concerns, it would be premature to conclude that memecoin activity is sabotaging Robinhood’s broader ambitions.

In fact, the silver lining here might be that speculation could be serving as an unexpected entry point into tokenized finance.

Though the company may have succeeded in getting traders through the door, whether they stay for tokenized stocks and RWAs, rather than the next viral memecoin, remains the bigger question on the table.

#4977. Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas has sued Kalshi, accusing the prediction market of offering an illegal, unregulated gambling platform to the state's residents, Attorney General Tim Griffin said on Wednesday.

The lawsuit, filed against KalshiEX LLC and Kalshi Inc., alleges multiple violations of the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act. The state is seeking restitution, penalties, taxes and an injunction.

Today I filed a lawsuit against @Kalshi for violating the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act by operating an unregulated gambling platform.

Kalshi has built a global business that it calls a prediction market, allowing consumers to wager money on the outcome of just about… pic.twitter.com/ePycNKXzy6

— Attorney General Tim Griffin (@AGTimGriffin) November 4, 2026

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said. "And gambling on an unregulated platform is squarely prohibited by Arkansas law."

Instead of obtaining licenses and paying the taxes required of gambling operators, Kalshi opened its platform to Arkansas residents as young as 18 and encouraged them to bet on sporting and entertainment events, Griffin alleged. "Arkansas law is concerned with substance, not semantics," he said.

Griffin pointed to Kalshi's own responsible-gambling tools, including self-exclusion, deposit limits and trading breaks, along with its partnership with the National Council on Problem Gambling, as evidence that the company knows its platform carries gambling-style risks. "Although Kalshi insists that its products are not gambling, its actions speak louder than its labels," he said.

Kalshi, which is regulated by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, has argued in other cases that its event contracts fall under exclusive federal jurisdiction and are not subject to state gambling laws.

Arkansas is the latest of more than a dozen states to take on prediction markets. Among the most significant cases:

Other states have also moved against prediction markets. Arizona filed criminal charges against Kalshi in March, a Washington court temporarily blocked its sports contracts in July, and Massachusetts' case is now before the state's highest court after a lower court barred Kalshi's sports contracts in January.

The CFTC has sued at least 10 states to defend what it says is its exclusive authority over prediction markets, and President Donald Trump has backed the agency, calling several state officials who challenged the platforms "scum."

Federal appeals courts are split. The Third Circuit ruled in April that New Jersey cannot regulate Kalshi's sports contracts, while the Sixth and Ninth Circuits sided with Ohio, Tennessee and Nevada. New Jersey has asked the Supreme Court to settle the question, backed by 39 other states and the District of Columbia, as well as the NFL. Kalshi's response to the petition is due November 9, and the Supreme Court is not expected to decide whether to take up the case until December at the earliest, ESPN reported.
